# RIDE operation catalog

Generated from `catalog/operations.psd1`. Edit catalog metadata, then run `tools/Export-RideCatalog.ps1`.

Names marked experimental identify preferences whose current feature behavior remains unverified. A Microsoft reference may document the feature rather than the exact registry mapping; read the description before selecting it. Registry apply/restore acceptance does not establish a visible feature effect. See the [post-migration review](migrations/settings-post-migration-review.md) for combined legacy tweaks and unresolved channels.

## Operations

| ID | Name | Category | Scope | Admin | Actions | Supported targets | Rollback | Description | Reference |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| application.edge-background-mode | Microsoft Edge background applications policy | Application settings / Microsoft Edge | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11 | Exact | Controls whether current Microsoft Edge can keep background applications running after its last browser window closes. Disabled writes 0; WindowsDefault removes the policy. This is an independently useful current replacement in the legacy preloading area; startup boost remains a separate control. Does not terminate existing processes or change Edge update scheduling. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/deployedge/microsoft-edge-policies/backgroundmodeenabled) |
| application.edge-install-desktop-shortcut | Microsoft Edge desktop shortcut creation upon installation | Application settings / Microsoft Edge | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11 | Exact | Controls the default desktop-shortcut creation behavior when Microsoft Edge is installed, for all channels without a channel-specific override. Disabled writes 0; WindowsDefault removes the policy. Requires Edge Update 1.3.128.0 or later and has no effect on an already installed Edge browser. Replaces the obsolete Explorer DisableEdgeDesktopShortcutCreation preference; does not delete existing shortcuts or suppress every later update action. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/deployedge/microsoft-edge-update-policies#createdesktopshortcutdefault) |
| application.edge-startup-boost | Microsoft Edge startup boost policy | Application settings / Microsoft Edge | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11 | Exact | Controls current Microsoft Edge startup boost, which prestarts browser processes at sign-in and restarts them after the browser closes. Disabled writes 0; WindowsDefault removes the policy. Replaces the obsolete EdgeHTML prelaunch area with a documented current control. Background apps use a separate policy; browser restart and managed-profile policy applicability require acceptance. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/deployedge/microsoft-edge-policies/startupboostenabled) |
| application.office-task-telemetry-fallback-2016 | Office 2016 telemetry fallback task | Application settings / Office | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11 | Exact | Controls the existing OfficeTelemetryAgentFallBack2016 task schedule. This identifies the fallback Office 2016 telemetry-agent task, not the logon task. Its current payload and installed-version applicability require acceptance; disabling it does not establish complete Office diagnostic-data suppression. The reference documents the task API. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/scheduledtasks/disable-scheduledtask?view=windowsserver2025-ps) |
| application.office-task-telemetry-logon-2016 | Office 2016 telemetry logon task | Application settings / Office | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11 | Exact | Controls the existing OfficeTelemetryAgentLogOn2016 task schedule. This identifies the Office 2016 telemetry-agent logon task, separately from its fallback task. Missing tasks are unavailable; current payload and Office diagnostic-policy interaction require acceptance. The reference documents the task API. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/scheduledtasks/disable-scheduledtask?view=windowsserver2025-ps) |
| application.office-task-click-to-run-monitor | Office Click-to-Run service monitoring | Application settings / Office | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11 | Exact | Controls the existing Office ClickToRun Service Monitor schedule. This is Office installation/service maintenance, so disabling it can affect maintenance rather than providing a general privacy outcome. It is unavailable without the matching task; the reference documents task enablement. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/scheduledtasks/disable-scheduledtask?view=windowsserver2025-ps) |
| powershell.powershell7-user-bin-path | PowerShell 7 existing user-bin startup PATH | Profile settings / PowerShell | User | No | Get, Test, Set, Restore | None (awaiting VM acceptance) | Exact | Optional current-user ConsoleHost startup block: Enabled appends the existing UserProfile/bin directory to the selected shell process PATH only when no normalized equivalent is present. Other PATH entries are preserved. No immediate or persistent environment change, directory creation, tool installation, WindowsApps repair or Terminal edit. Default/unset remove only this managed block; saved-run restore recovers captured profile content. Existing processes and inherited PATH are not rewritten. Known unmarked legacy UserBinaries code requires review before enabling. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.core/about/about_profiles) |
| powershell.powershell7-terminal-colors | PowerShell 7 session colors | Profile settings / PowerShell | User | No | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11, Windows Server 2025 | Exact | Manage a current-user ConsoleHost startup block. Recommended applies One Half Dark in normal Windows Terminal sessions and Campbell PowerShell blue when the actual shell token is elevated. AdminBlue changes only elevated sessions. Default removes only this RIDE block; restore recovers captured profile content. Does not install PowerShell, edit Terminal settings, PATH, execution policy or tab titles. Existing sessions and other shells are unaffected. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.core/about/about_profiles) |
| powershell.windows-powershell51-user-bin-path | Windows PowerShell 5.1 existing user-bin startup PATH | Profile settings / PowerShell | User | No | Get, Test, Set, Restore | None (awaiting VM acceptance) | Exact | Optional current-user ConsoleHost startup block: Enabled appends the existing UserProfile/bin directory to the selected shell process PATH only when no normalized equivalent is present. Other PATH entries are preserved. No immediate or persistent environment change, directory creation, tool installation, WindowsApps repair or Terminal edit. Default/unset remove only this managed block; saved-run restore recovers captured profile content. Existing processes and inherited PATH are not rewritten. Known unmarked legacy UserBinaries code requires review before enabling. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.core/about/about_profiles) |
| powershell.windows-powershell51-terminal-colors | Windows PowerShell 5.1 session colors | Profile settings / PowerShell | User | No | Get, Test, Set, Restore | None (awaiting VM acceptance) | Exact | Manage a current-user ConsoleHost startup block. | package.ntcore-4gb-patch | NTCore 4GB Patch (portable candidate) | Software / Analysis utilities | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Download, Install, Uninstall, Restore | None (awaiting VM acceptance) | Exact | Stage the pinned x86 patch utility; installation does not open or modify target executables. Direct invocation is unsafe when its .Backup path is occupied. Use the RIDE guarded launcher, which creates an exact original-byte backup before execution, refuses a pre-existing backup, and verifies that only the PE large-address-aware flag changed. Windows 11 acceptance of the guarded workflow remains pending. Retained 2007 copyright metadata says all rights reserved; no product-specific licence grant was found. The exact-version local review is informational and does not block migration. | [Product info](https://ntcore.com/4gb-patch/) |

| package.docker-desktop | Docker Desktop | Software / Development Tools | User | No | Get, Test, Download, Install, Uninstall, Restore | None (awaiting VM acceptance) | Compensating | Candidate to install or remove official x64 Docker Desktop on vendor-supported Windows 11 editions, either per-user without elevation or for all users with elevation. RIDE lifecycle and recovery acceptance for both variants is pending; no Windows target is supported yet. All-users mode adds the publisher privileged service and supports Windows containers; per-user mode is the recommended Linux-container setup. Removal uses the publisher keep-data option to preserve Docker resources. Requires Windows 11 Enterprise, Pro, or Education version 23H2 (build 22631) or later, WSL 2 version 2.1.5 or later, hardware virtualization, SLAT, and at least 8 GB RAM. RIDE does not install or update WSL, enable Windows features, choose a container backend, accept Docker terms, start Docker Desktop, or provision containers. Docker Desktop is not supported on Windows Server. | [Product info](https://www.docker.com/products/docker-desktop/) |

| windows.local-password-maximum-age | Local-account maximum password age (experimental) | Windows settings / Accounts | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | None (awaiting VM acceptance) | Exact | Advanced machine/admin maximum password age for the local SAM account authority. NoExpiry means Never expires; Days1 through Days999 are explicit day choices from a bounded catalog range, checked against the current minimum age. Uses local NetUserModalsSet level 1002 to change only maximum-age seconds, with complete nonsecret modal context capture, local SAM SID identity, drift checks and exact prior raw-seconds recovery. Domain controllers and unknown computer roles are refused. No password, per-account never-expire flag, complexity, minimum age, length, history, lockout or domain policy is changed. The API reports currently applied local SAM data, not local GPO definitions or managed source; Not defined/removal is not invented. No universal 42-day/default preset or recommended-profile weakening. | windows.python-manager-automatic-install | Disable Python manager implicit runtime installs (candidate) | Windows settings / Accounts and configuration | User | No | Get, Test, Set, Restore | None (awaiting VM acceptance) | Exact | Disabled writes the current user Environment value PYTHON_MANAGER_AUTOMATIC_INSTALL=false, which the Python install manager reads to prevent automatic runtime installation through py exec and its no-runtime launch path. Existing processes may retain stale environment data until restarted or refreshed. This does not install/update the manager or a runtime, change PATH/aliases, or disable the manager's own update behavior. Disabling can affect network-status detection and applications. Microsoft documents connectivity probing; registry behavior and network results require separate target review. | windows.remote-assistance-policy | Remote Assistance request permission | Windows settings / Network | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11 | Exact | Allow or disallow users from requesting Remote Assistance by setting the documented fAllowToGetHelp behavior value; Quick Assist is managed separately. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/customize/desktop/unattend/microsoft-windows-remoteassistance-exe-fallowtogethelp) |
| windows.remote-desktop-firewall | Remote Desktop local inbound firewall rules | Windows settings / Network | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11 | Exact | Enables or disables existing local RemoteDesktop-* firewall rules in PersistentStore. Captures each individual flag and never creates missing rules. Does not enable Remote Desktop connections, change authentication or override domain firewall policy. Use with the separate connection permission when composing a Remote Desktop outcome. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/remote/remote-desktop-services/remotepc/remote-desktop-allow-access) |
| windows.smb1-server-protocol | SMB 1 server protocol | Windows settings / Network | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11 | Exact | Controls only the EnableSMB1Protocol server flag when its srv driver service is present. An absent driver is unavailable; no optional feature is installed. Enabling SMB1 can expose an obsolete insecure protocol. SMB2/3, client components, sharing bindings and firewall rules are separate. The legacy EnableSMBServer reverse selector leaves SMB1 unchanged; restore recovers the captured flag. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/storage/file-server/troubleshoot/detect-enable-and-disable-smbv1-v2-v3) |
| windows.smb2-server-protocol | SMB 2 and SMB 3 server protocols | Windows settings / Network | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11 | Exact | Controls the EnableSMB2Protocol server flag, which governs both SMB2 and SMB3. Disabling it can prevent modern SMB clients from reaching shares. SMB1, client components, sharing bindings and firewall rules remain separate. No optional feature is installed; restore recovers the captured flag. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/storage/file-server/troubleshoot/detect-enable-and-disable-smbv1-v2-v3) |
| windows.unidentified-network-category | Unidentified network location policy (experimental) | Windows settings / Network | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11 | Exact | Retain the legacy unidentified-network policy signature and Public/Private values. Microsoft reference explains the network area, not this fixed signature mapping. Current Network List Manager behavior remains unverified; does not recategorize every active adapter. WindowsDefault removes this override; saved-run restore recovers the prior value and type. No restart or application refresh is performed. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/security/threat-protection/security-policy-settings/network-list-manager-policies#unidentified-networks) |
| windows.winhttp-wpad-policy | WinHTTP WPAD detection | Windows settings / Network | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11, Windows Server 2025 | Exact | Disable WinHTTP WPAD detection using the documented machine registry value; this does not disable the WinHTTP Auto-Proxy service or proxy auto-discovery in every application. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/troubleshoot/windows-server/networking/disable-http-proxy-auth-features#how-to-disable-wpad) |
| windows.adapter-microsoft-network-client-binding | Client for Microsoft Networks adapter binding | Windows settings / Network bindings | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11 | Exact | Enables or disables Client for Microsoft Networks (ms_msclient) on currently visible adapters. This binding supports accessing Microsoft network resources; it is distinct from the server sharing binding and SMB protocol flags. Stable GUIDs and coupled flags are captured for restore. Peer access may be interrupted; new adapters remain untouched. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/netadapter/set-netadapterbinding) |
| windows.adapter-file-printer-sharing-binding | File and Printer Sharing for Microsoft Networks adapter binding | Windows settings / Network bindings | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11 | Exact | Enables or disables File and Printer Sharing for Microsoft Networks (ms_server) on currently visible adapters. This is the server sharing binding; SMB protocol versions, firewall rules and installed components remain separate. Stable adapter GUIDs and coupled flags are captured for restore; new adapters remain untouched. Real peer sharing requires acceptance. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/netadapter/set-netadapterbinding) |
| windows.adapter-ipv4-binding | IPv4 transport adapter binding | Windows settings / Network bindings | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11 | Exact | Enables or disables Internet Protocol Version 4 (ms_tcpip) on currently visible adapters. Disabling it can immediately interrupt IPv4 connectivity, including remote management. Stable adapter GUIDs and coupled flags are captured for local restore; new adapters remain untouched. IPv6 and SMB server protocol flags are separate controls. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/netadapter/set-netadapterbinding) |
| windows.adapter-ipv6-binding | IPv6 transport adapter binding | Windows settings / Network bindings | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11 | Exact | Enables or disables Internet Protocol Version 6 (ms_tcpip6) on currently visible adapters. Disabling it can interrupt IPv6 connectivity and dependent Windows features; this binding flag does not disable every IPv6 subsystem. Stable adapter GUIDs and coupled flags are captured for local restore; new adapters remain untouched. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/netadapter/set-netadapterbinding) |
| windows.adapter-lldp-binding | Link Layer Discovery Protocol adapter binding | Windows settings / Network bindings | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11 | Exact | Enables or disables the Microsoft Link Layer Discovery Protocol binding (ms_lldp) on currently visible adapters. This controls the LLDP binding rather than the separate LLTD topology mapper/responder. Capture includes stable adapter GUIDs and coupled flags for restore; new adapters remain untouched. Actual neighbor discovery requires peer acceptance. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/netadapter/set-netadapterbinding) |
| windows.adapter-lltd-binding | Link Layer Topology Discovery adapter binding | Windows settings / Network bindings | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11 | Exact | Enables or disables Link Layer Topology Discovery mapper (ms_lltdio) and responder (ms_rspndr) together on currently visible adapters. They implement one topology-discovery feature. Stable adapter GUIDs and coupled flags are captured for restore; new adapters remain untouched. Missing components and actual topology behavior require acceptance. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/netadapter/set-netadapterbinding) |
| windows.adapter-qos-packet-scheduler-binding | QoS packet scheduler adapter binding | Windows settings / Network bindings | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11 | Exact | Enables or disables QoS Packet Scheduler (ms_pacer) on currently visible adapters. It controls the scheduler binding, not a promised bandwidth increase or a universal reservation percentage. Stable adapter GUIDs and coupled flags are captured for restore; new adapters remain untouched. Actual traffic behavior requires acceptance. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/netadapter/set-netadapterbinding) |
| windows.application-inventory-policy | Application compatibility inventory policy | Windows settings / Policy | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11 | Exact | Controls the application compatibility inventory collector for installed applications, files, devices and drivers. Disabled writes DisableInventory=1; WindowsDefault removes the override. CEIP and diagnostic-data policy can independently affect collection. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/client-management/mdm/policy-csp-admx-appcompat#appcompatturnoffprograminventory) |
| windows.application-impact-telemetry | Application impact telemetry policy | Windows settings / Policy | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11 | Exact | Controls application-impact telemetry for Windows components used by applications. Disabled writes AITEnable=0; WindowsDefault removes the override. CEIP policy can suppress this independently; restart or new application activity may be required. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/client-management/mdm/policy-csp-admx-appcompat#appcompatturnoffapplicationimpacttelemetry) |
| windows.secure-attention-logon | Ctrl+Alt+Delete requirement before sign-in | Windows settings / Policy | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11 | Exact | Required writes DisableCAD=0, requiring the secure attention sequence before interactive sign-in. NotRequired writes 1. WindowsDefault removes the override; domain policy can take precedence. The policy reference describes sign-in behavior; test the actual sign-in screen separately. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/client-management/mdm/policy-csp-localpoliciessecurityoptions#interactivelogon_donotrequirectrlaltdel) |
| windows.ncsi-active-probe-policy | NCSI active connectivity tests policy | Windows settings / Policy | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11 | Exact | Controls the policy override for NCSI active Internet connectivity tests. Disabled writes NoActiveProbe=1; Enabled writes 0. WindowsDefault removes the policy override. The separate NlaSvc EnableActiveProbing preference and managed policy can affect the result. Disabling probes can disrupt application connectivity detection. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/troubleshoot/windows-client/networking/internet-explorer-edge-open-connect-corporate-public-network) |
| windows.remote-desktop-connections | Remote Desktop connection permission | Windows settings / Policy | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11 | Exact | Allows or denies incoming Remote Desktop connections. Enabled writes fDenyTSConnections=0; Disabled writes 1. WindowsDefault removes the override. Requires a supported Windows edition and service; firewall rules, NLA and managed policy remain separate. The feature reference explains access prerequisites; no credentials or authentication policy are changed. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/remote/remote-desktop-services/remotepc/remote-desktop-allow-access) |
| windows.start-most-used-policy | Start menu most-used application list policy | Windows settings / Policy | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11 | Exact | Hides the frequently used application list in Start with Hidden=1; Allowed=0 explicitly permits it. WindowsDefault removes the policy override. Tracking preferences and other policies can still prevent the list from appearing. Restart or shell refresh and current Start-layout acceptance remain separate. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/client-management/mdm/policy-csp-start#hidefrequentlyusedapps) |
| windows.active-power-scheme | Active existing Windows power scheme | Windows settings / Power | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11 | Exact | Selects an existing Balanced or High Performance scheme by stable GUID without parsing localized names. Exact GUID queries inspect schemes even when omitted from the list; device capabilities or policy can still prevent activation. Does not create, duplicate or delete schemes. Ultimate Performance selection and scheme creation remain deferred. Apply and restore verify the active GUID; a failed activation is reported. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/design/device-experiences/powercfg-command-line-options) |
| windows.owned-balanced-power-plan | Balanced power-plan owned copy (experimental) | Windows settings / Power | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | None (awaiting VM acceptance) | Compensating | Advanced explicit creation of one separately owned Balanced template copy using exact source/destination GUIDs. Never selects it or repairs existing selection. Source availability is conditional; no name matching, fallback or adoption. Ownership intent is saved before duplication and finalized with a native export fingerprint. Recovery only deletes the same unchanged inactive owned copy after selection recovery; incomplete, changed, active or orphaned copies require review. Native export reproducibility, hardware/edition, GUI and target acceptance remain pending; normal apply is blocked. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/design/device-experiences/powercfg-command-line-options#duplicatescheme) |

Unsupported hardware or schemes report Unavailable. Does not change the active scheme, hibernation or unrelated indexes. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/customize/power-settings/display-settings-display-idle-timeout) |

| windows.defender-cloud-membership | Cloud-delivered protection (Microsoft Defender Antivirus) | Windows settings / Security | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11 | Exact | Configure cloud-delivered protection independently of automatic sample submission. Advanced enables the legacy MAPS reporting choice; Basic and Advanced have the same effect on Windows 11. This is built-in Defender Antivirus protection, with no separate Microsoft 365 or Defender for Endpoint subscription. Local configuration procedures imply no Microsoft-account sign-in, paid membership registration or Endpoint onboarding requirement; local-account VM acceptance remains pending. Operational Defender Antivirus and Microsoft cloud connectivity are required. Tamper protection and managed policy may block or override changes; inspecting this registry value does not prove effective protection. WindowsDefault removes only this override; saved-run restore recovers its prior value and type. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/client-management/mdm/policy-csp-defender#allowcloudprotection) |

No restart or application refresh is performed. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/client-management/mdm/policy-csp-defender#submitsamplesconsent) |

| windows.firewall-all-profiles | Enable Windows Firewall for all network types | Windows settings / Security | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | None (awaiting VM acceptance) | Exact | Configure only local firewall Enabled in PersistentStore for the declared network types. | windows.wap-push-service | Device Management WAP Push service | Windows settings / Services | Machine | Yes | Get, Test, Set, Restore | Windows 11 | Exact | Advanced opt-in service control. AutomaticRunning sets Automatic and starts now while preserving delay; Disabled stops and disables while preserving the dormant delay. AutomaticDelayedRunning selects Automatic (Delayed Start) and starts now. Startup-only AutomaticStartup, AutomaticDelayedStartup, ManualStartup and DisabledStartup preserve runtime. Boot scheduling applies next boot with no fixed delay guarantee. Device-management workloads may depend on this service. Baseline AutomaticRunning is a RIDE choice, not a Windows default. Saved restore captures startup/runtime, SCM delay and literal delay value/type/absence. Missing services and failed stages are explicit. New choices await GUI/SCM/boot/workload VM acceptance; no Server support is added. | [Microsoft docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winsvc/ns-winsvc-service_delayed_auto_start_info) |

## Setting scope, behavior and requirements

Each operation ID fixes its scope. Administrator rights do not determine who receives a setting, whether it is enforced, or whether its GUI is editable. User operations use the execution account; alternate-administrator execution does not select the interactive user. Presentation metadata is additive to catalog schema 1 and does not change states, handlers or saved-run recovery. Missing metadata remains unverified, including in external libraries.

These are declared configuration effects. Effective-policy inspection is handler-specific: modern firewall candidates distinguish local and ActiveStore state and show source when available. Registry values alone do not establish effective policy or its source, and a declared platform default does not prove active enforcement. GUI and target acceptance are tracked separately in the post-migration review. Requirements describe the setting, rather than subscriptions needed only for optional cloud management.

NoProfile or blocked startup bypasses the block.<br>CurrentUserCurrentHost PowerShell profile; process PATH after startup | Selected runtime installed and profile execution permitted; Existing current-user bin directory; provision and review its contents separately; Review unmarked legacy startup writers before enabling; native Windows 11 acceptance pending | Default/unset remove the block; Baseline selects Default; Saved-run restore recovers profile content, not environment changes in running or child processes; Persistent user/machine PATH, directory provisioning and WindowsApps repair remain separate | [Reference](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.core/about/about_profiles)<br>[Reference](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.core/about/about_environment_variables) |
| powershell.windows-powershell51-terminal-colors | Current user; Windows PowerShell 5.1 ConsoleHost startup in Windows Terminal | Preference | New normal sessions use Campbell; elevated sessions use blue. NoProfile and blocked startup scripts bypass this setting.<br>CurrentUserCurrentHost PowerShell startup profile; Terminal settings are preserved | Installed Windows PowerShell 5.1 and Windows Terminal; profile execution permitted; Apply in the intended user context; another administrator account has its own profile; Server requires Desktop Experience; cloud hydration remains unverified | AdminBlue preserves normal-session appearance; Default/unset remove the RIDE block; Baseline selects Default; Saved-run restore recovers the prior managed content and preserves unrelated edits | [Reference](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.core/about/about_profiles)<br>[Reference](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/terminal/customize-settings/color-schemes)<br>[Reference](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/terminal/customize-settings/profile-general#automatically-run-as-administrator) |

| windows.3d-objects-folder-shell-policy | Machine, elevation required: 3D Objects shell-folder visibility preferences in both registry views | Unverified | Hidden writes ThisPCPolicy=Hide and Visible writes Show in the native and 32-bit 3D Objects property bags together. The name does not establish an enforced policy; actual shell visibility is unverified.<br>File Explorer and file dialogs: 3D Objects visibility (unverified current-build mapping) | Microsoft describes known folders, not these ThisPCPolicy values. Validate both registry views, current Explorer/dialog behavior and conflicting namespace registrations.; No folder content, location, permissions or Home pins are changed; no shell refresh occurs. Keep experimental mappings outside recommended profiles until acceptance. | Legacy Show3DObjectsInExplorer removed both overrides; WindowsDefault matches that action. Visible writes explicit Show.; Exact saved restore recovers each prior value/type/absence, including partial states; it is not the same as the separate namespace registration operation. | [Reference](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/shell/known-folders) |
| windows.3d-objects-folder-this-pc | Machine, elevation required: 3D Objects namespace registration in This PC | Feature configuration | Hidden deletes the declared 3D Objects namespace registry subtrees; Visible creates missing empty registration roots. Inspect the target shell and both Explorer views before relying on visibility.; No Explorer restart or folder relocation occurs. The RIDE Visible baseline does not establish a Windows default. | Legacy hide/show removed/created the same registration roots; Visible cannot reconstruct previously deleted registry values.; Exact saved restore recovers captured subtrees, typed values and security. Shell-folder ThisPCPolicy preferences remain separate. | [Reference](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/shell/nse-junction) |

| windows.action-center-policy | Current Windows execution user: Notification Center visibility | Enforced policy | Disabled writes DisableNotificationCenter=1; published Enabled removes the override. | windows.adapter-file-printer-sharing-binding | Machine, elevation required: File and Printer Sharing on currently visible adapters | Feature configuration | Enabled/Disabled sets ms_server on the captured adapters, affecting the adapter server-sharing binding. Microsoft documents binding configuration and coupled changes; actual device and peer behavior remains a separate check.; Legacy DisableSMBServer also disabled SMB1 and SMB2/3; this binding alone does not reproduce that bundle. | Legacy selectors addressed wildcard adapters; RIDE captures stable GUIDs and coupled flags for saved recovery. Newly added adapters are not part of that restore.; Restore recovers captured binding flags, not interrupted connections or remote sessions. Verify local recovery before changing a management interface. | [Reference](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/netadapter/set-netadapterbinding?view=windowsserver2025-ps) |

The SystemServices policy reference documents startup modes, not live runtime or complete peer discovery; firewall and network profile remain separate.; Service must exist; dependencies, managed policy and transient states can prevent transitions. Existing supported targets are unchanged; no new GUI, boot or workload acceptance is claimed. | Legacy EnableSSDPdiscovery selected Manual and started now, matching current Enabled intent. Manual startup permits demand start; a later trigger/runtime change is not a captured-state restore.; There is no unset/override-removal state. Restore the saved run for prior startup/runtime; an explicit preset or RIDE baseline is not a Windows default. Recovery does not undo effects in workloads or unowned dependencies. | [Reference](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/client-management/mdm/policy-csp-systemservices#configuressdpdiscoveryservicestartupmode) |
| windows.start-most-used-policy | Machine, elevation required: Frequently used applications in Start | Enforced policy | Hidden writes NoStartMenuMFUprogramsList=1; Allowed writes 0; WindowsDefault removes it.<br>Computer Configuration > Administrative Templates > Start Menu and Taskbar > Remove frequent programs list from the Start Menu | Microsoft documents the policy mapping. Usage tracking, other policies and current Start layout can still suppress the list.; No Explorer restart, sign-out, pin change or application launch occurs. Retain existing target declarations and verify actual UI separately. | Legacy ShowMostUsedApps removed the override. WindowsDefault matches removal; Allowed=0 explicitly permits the list but does not populate it.; Exact saved restore recovers registry type/value/absence. Removing an override leaves the effective choice to Windows and other controls. | [Reference](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/client-management/mdm/policy-csp-start#hidefrequentlyusedapps) |
| windows.start-recently-added-apps | Machine, elevation required: Recently added applications in Start | Enforced policy | Hidden writes HideRecentlyAddedApps=1; UserChoice writes 0; WindowsDefault removes the override.<br>Settings > Personalization > Start; related Remove Recently added list policy | Microsoft documents this exact policy, supported editions and shell/restart requirements. No restart or shell termination is performed.; No Explorer restart, sign-out, pin change or application launch occurs. Retain existing target declarations and verify actual UI separately. | Legacy ShowRecentlyAddedApps removed the override. WindowsDefault matches; UserChoice=0 is explicit.; Exact saved restore recovers registry type/value/absence. Removing an override leaves the effective choice to Windows and other controls. | [Reference](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/client-management/mdm/policy-csp-start#hiderecentlyaddedapps) |
| windows.start-run-as-different-user | Current Windows execution user: Run as different user in Start | Enforced policy | Enabled writes ShowRunAsDifferentUserInStart=1; Disabled writes 0; WindowsDefault removes it.<br>User Configuration > Administrative Templates > Start Menu and Taskbar > Show Run as different user command on Start | Microsoft documents user scope and supporting applications. This does not store credentials or launch another identity.; No Explorer restart, sign-out, pin change or application launch occurs. Retain existing target declarations and verify actual UI separately. | Legacy wrote/removed the same value in HKLM; the maintained operation intentionally uses documented HKCU. | windows.startup-sound | Device: Windows startup sound preference, shared across users | Preference | Enabled writes DisableStartupSound=0; Disabled writes 1. This changes only the stored machine preference. Actual startup playback and checkbox editability depend on applicable policy, audio availability and target acceptance.<br>Control Panel > Sound > Sounds > Play Windows Startup sound (related UI; current mapping needs acceptance) | Windows 11; administrator rights required for this machine registry value, independently of the privileges of the related GUI; The themes reference documents sound personalization, not this registry value or startup checkbox. Boot, GUI and managed-policy behavior remain pending | Baseline explicitly selects Enabled; it does not reproduce the catalog-declared absent literal default or prove effective playback; No unset state is declared. Saved-run restore recovers the captured prior value, type or absence; sound-scheme selection and playback-device mute are separate | [Reference](https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/experience/personalization/personalize-your-windows-experience-with-themes) |

Each named state coordinates the values listed below. WindowsDefault removes the overrides; saved-run restore recovers the captured data, type and absence of each value. Platform-defined defaults are not inferred from the baseline.

## Managed settings

These settings use Windows management cmdlets, powercfg or NetBIOS/sound registry preferences. The NetBIOS and sound references document feature behavior, not their registry mappings. No baseline or universal clean-image value is assumed. Scheduled tasks must already exist. Adapter recovery captures coupled flags by adapter GUID. Power scheme selection does not create a missing scheme. Refer to the migration review for omitted legacy bundle behavior.

## PowerShell profile settings

Optional current-user ConsoleHost startup blocks; see [profile architecture and recovery](powershell-profiles.md). Default/unset remove only the managed block; Baseline resolves to Default. Saved-run restore recovers its captured predecessor. These are fixed reviewed RIDE presets, not edits to Terminal settings.json. show/status inspect configuration rather than observing another session. UserBinPath appends an existing user bin directory only at selected-shell startup, without changing persistent PATH or provisioning files. Pending entries cannot be applied until applicable disposable target and runtime acceptance is recorded.

| Operation | Runtime | Setting | States | Normal Recommended palette | Elevated palette |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| powershell.powershell7-terminal-colors | PowerShell7 | TerminalColors | AdminBlue, Default, Recommended; Baseline alias | One Half Dark | Campbell PowerShell |
| powershell.powershell7-user-bin-path | PowerShell7 | UserBinPath | Default, Enabled; Baseline alias | Not applicable | Not applicable |
| powershell.windows-powershell51-terminal-colors | WindowsPowerShell51 | TerminalColors | AdminBlue, Default, Recommended; Baseline alias | Campbell | Campbell PowerShell |
| powershell.windows-powershell51-user-bin-path | WindowsPowerShell51 | UserBinPath | Default, Enabled; Baseline alias | Not applicable | Not applicable |

## Package variants

Flat package entries retain their installed default. Explicit variants never fall back. Portable packages own only manifest-listed files and preserve modified and user-created content.

## Registered uninstallers

An explicit uninstaller format may differ from the installer format for a publisher wrapper. Removal still uses the registered command and declared quiet arguments.

- `package.firefox` - installer: Msi; uninstaller: Exe
- `package.phoenix-protector` - installer: Msi; uninstaller: Msi
- `package.tmog` - installer: Exe; uninstaller: Exe

## Desktop application applicability

The following products require Windows Server Desktop Experience. Server Core is explicitly unavailable; target lifecycle evidence remains in the application ledger. Portable file deployment does not establish application or forensic behavior.
